# City QA & Direction — NinjaOne Capital

> **Resuming a thread? Read `STATE.md` instead** — it is the ~55-line current-state file, rewritten each tick. This file is the append-only permanent record of the task queue and every verdict; it is for looking up specific history, not for reconstructing where things stand.

Maintained by Claude (director). Codex responds inline under each item. Only Steve accepts work.

## Current direction — 2026-08-21

**DIRECTION RESET.** Steve has terminated the plate-carving approach for the city and delegated city (L4) direction to the director. All patch lanes on the baked plate are **closed**: I-series station repairs (including the in-flight I24 crop work), LFX land fixes, CFX detail overlays, and D01–D06 cutout/socket calibration. Do not open new ones; do not extend existing ones.

What survives from the current state:

- The currently mounted city runtime stays as-is. It is the visible placeholder until the v2 pipeline produces a better district. Do not spend further effort improving it.
- The master plate, the registered package anchors, the district masks, and the native-foliage reuse mechanism are inputs to the v2 pipeline (see AGENTS.md).
- L1–L3 and the camera/LoD framework are frozen — escalate to Steve for any change.

Rationale (short form): a monolithic baked plate cannot be decomposed into progressive per-node LoD; five weeks of ledger evidence (I17–I21, LFX01–LFX06, the rejected close cutout cohorts, persistent close-tier softness) demonstrates this structurally. The city is rebuilt bottom-up from a kit of parts, composed deterministically. Full pipeline: AGENTS.md, "City pipeline v2."

## Task queue

### T1 — Grammar extraction (OPEN)

Build a deterministic script (e.g. `scripts/build-ninjaone-city-grammar-r1.mjs`) that derives from the master plate and the existing registered layout (`city-master-node-layout-r3.json`, district masks):

- circulation graph: roads, stairs, bridges as polylines with approximate widths;
- building footprints with size-class buckets, plus a density map;
- terrace/contour bands;
- palette: dominant swatches per material family (roof, wall, rock, foliage, water).

Output: one JSON grammar under `public/career-world/capitals/ninjaone/city-v2/grammar/` plus PNG debug overlays (graph drawn over the plate; footprints drawn over the plate) under `.codex-tmp/qa/T1/`.

Constraints: no runtime changes, no new tests beyond property checks on the script's own output (e.g. graph connectivity, footprints within district masks). No ImageGen.

Acceptance: director reviews the overlays for fidelity to the master read.

**Codex:** T1 candidate is ready for director review. `scripts/build-ninjaone-city-grammar-r1.mjs` deterministically writes `public/career-world/capitals/ninjaone/city-v2/grammar/ninjaone-city-grammar-r1.json` and the two required overlays; `npm run check:ninjaone-city-grammar` reproduces them byte-for-byte while checking graph connectivity, required edge kinds, district-contained footprints/contours, and five populated palette families. The candidate contains 31 circulation edges (14 road, 9 stairs, 8 bridge), 45 building footprints across four derived size classes, a 12x9 density map, 18 terrace bands, and roof/wall/rock/foliage/water swatches. No runtime, L1-L3, camera, LoD, or ImageGen changes were made. Visual fidelity remains a director decision.

**Director verdict (2026-08-21 12:30): PARTIAL — revise as r2.** Machinery, determinism, and property checks are the right shape. Fidelity fails the acceptance bar in four ways: (1) landmark structures are missed or under-captured — the palace crown complex, the glass train hall, and the statue observatory have no true-extent footprints; (2) 45 near-uniform stamps vs ~150+ visible structures, with several boxes on pure vegetation; (3) circulation coverage is sparse — the east ridge and right-side industrial district are nearly empty; (4) several bridge edges are abstract diagonals across rooftops rather than traces of real spans. R2 requirements are specified in the dispatched packet (`.codex-tmp/qa/T1/packet-t1-r2.md`): landmark size class with true extents (use `city-master-node-layout-r3.json` as a prior), ≥100 structure-dominant footprints with vegetation exclusion, ≥70% of sampled polyline points on circulation-like pixels with densified segments, bridges only across visible water/ravine spans, east-ridge + industrial coverage, r2 outputs alongside r1.

Therefore no r2 outputs, counts, or validation statistics are claimed; rerun `npm run build:ninjaone-city-grammar`, `npm run check:ninjaone-city-grammar`, and lint in an environment allowed to execute the installed Node binary before visual review.

### T2 — Kit-of-parts contract (OPEN — begins after T1 review)

Define the kit taxonomy and the per-class generation contract template: projection, light, palette, style-reference crop, socket/baseline standard, two-candidate limit, quarantine-only output. **No generation calls in this task.** Deliverable: `docs/career-world/CITY-KIT-CONTRACT.md`.

Full-capital taxonomy targets 14 ground/terrace entries (slab, retaining wall, cliff transition), 19 circulation entries (road straight/curve/junction, stairs, bridge), 48 building entries across grammar classes `compact`/`standard`/`large` plus `landmark` and the 19 named skills, 16 props, and zero new foliage entries because registered L2 conifer atlases are reuse-first. ### T3 — D05 composer proof (UNBLOCKED — staged)

Composer assembles D05 (Western Skill Terraces) from kit + grammar, graded against the master's D05 crop at the standard capital/site/close cameras. Defined in detail after T2.

No right-hand D05 bend was detected in the current consolidated runs, so the absent right-hand curve is a library limitation, not a demonstrated D05 blocker.

**OWNER LIVE REVIEW of the mounted pyramid (Steve, 2026-08-25 night; full free-camera flight, all zooms) — director triage.** Overall verdict positive ("vibrant and honestly fantastic"; shimmer "pretty slick"; the organic headland cottage flagged as a keeper quality for future district packets). Fifteen observations, triaged:

- **CLASS A — hero composite not integrated (SYSTEMATIC; 3 sightings: S15 floating/overlapping, S18/S11 edges overlapping baked structures, explicit "100% placed over a baked building").** Root cause: T9b composited sprites directly OVER the canon without the erase-under step the T4d-r3 scaffold recipe used — and the canon tiles had themselves repainted B's heroes, so mismatched under-paint edges protrude around the sprites. Director owns this spec gap (the packet said "reuse the T9a recipe verbatim," which never handled heroes). Fix: regenerate the hero-bearing tiles from sprite-composited scaffolds (erase painted hero → composite sprite → tile generation paints ground to it — the proven anchor-and-cover mechanics at tile scale), re-stitch, re-derive, remount. Est. 3–4 calls. TOP PRIORITY.
- **CLASS B — max-zoom softness + ghosting (fabric blurry vs heroes at the floor; double-exposure reads in overlap bands).** Two contributors: the current zoom floor stretches the canon ~1.5x (heroes survive stretch better — downsampled sprite linework), and the 120px smoothstep feather ghosts at stretch exactly as T7c predicted. Fixes: (i) zoom-floor decision — honest cap at canon 1:1 (max zoom becomes ~1/3 shallower, everything tack-sharp) vs keep the deep floor and accept mild stretch; frozen camera policy, Steve's call; (ii) re-stitch with a tighter feather (~24–40px) in architecture zones — deterministic, zero ImageGen, judged by eye.
- **CLASS C — paint-level composition defects:** chapel with a cutoff edge (check against the overlap map — may be a stitch casualty resolved by the Class B re-stitch), S10 lacking a base entrance, the shore house with a see-through hole, a walkway dead-ending into a retaining wall. Targeted single-tile re-rolls with defect-specific prompt notes; batch with Class D.
- **CLASS D — shore refinement (standing debt, reconfirmed):** shoreline polish + shore buildings; batch with Class C as one polish pass.
- **CLASS E — shimmer catching mossy masonry:** classifier tweak (exclude grey-green range / widen standoff on wall tops); deterministic.
- **CLASS F — crane arm fading at the district boundary (mask feather through protruding architecture):** ACCEPT FOR NOW — the neighboring district's regeneration covers it; carving the mask around hero protrusions is available if it grates before then.

Proposed order: A (hero re-integration lane) → B (Steve's zoom-floor ruling + free re-stitch experiment) → E (quick) → C+D as one owner-briefed polish pass → F parked. Awaiting Steve's approval of the plan and the Class B zoom ruling.

**Director F20 on T10b (2026-08-26): PASS.** At the new floor the correct tier serves at or below native resolution (verified live via DOM at multiple depths: capital tier at shallow, intermediate mid, tier switching as specced), no stretch zone remains, and the zoom wheel stops at the art's edge. Shimmer at 9.09% with zero architecture violations. One refinement candidate recorded, not a blocker: the floor is SPAN-based, so its effective px-per-master scales with viewport width — on narrow panes max zoom lands safely below canon 1:1, but a very wide monitor (≳2600 CSS px viewport) would push past 1:1 and reintroduce mild stretch. A resolution-aware floor (cap px-per-master directly) is the clean future form; queue with the next camera-policy touch.

**Codex (T3c-r4b):** Current-worktree verification found no source-pixel/rounded-destination placement loop to quote: the active resampler is already Sharp destination-space sampling, `const { data, info } = await sharp(kit.imageBytes).resize(width, height, { fit: "fill", kernel: "lanczos3" }).ensureAlpha().raw().toBuffer({ resolveWithObject: true });`. The packet's stated historical defect (source rows written to rounded destination rows) is therefore not present in this checkout; I retained that destination-space path rather than fabricate or reintroduce a loop. The corrected mechanical gap was the r4 existence-only property: the old assertion was `assert(preparedOpaquePixels > 0 && placedOpaquePixels > 0 && gradedOpaquePixels > 0, ...)`. r4b replaces it with a per-placed-building solidity property: nearest-map the source alpha=255 mask to the exact Sharp-rendered dimensions and require nontransparent rendered coverage >=0.98 of that mapped coverage. Named results: S18 `90768/90776 = 0.999912`; S01 `77023/77030 = 0.999909`; S15 `89070/89078 = 0.999910`; S10 `69833/69843 = 0.999857`; S11 `49725/49730 = 0.999899`; all 19 placed buildings pass. Bundled Node syntax/build/check and `git diff --check` pass. Proofs: `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-composed-ungraded-r4b.png`, `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-composed-graded-r4b.png`, `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-composed-capital-scale-r4b.png`, `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-composed-vs-master-r4b.png`, `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-composed-clean-standalone-r4b.png`, and `.codex-tmp/qa/T3/t3c-r4b/d05-placements-r4b.json`. No runtime, frozen-layer, kit, ImageGen, or commit change.

**Codex (T3c-r4c):** The striped pass is the D05 district-mask clip. The input is the correct full-context D05 mask, not `city-progressive-water-exclusion-mask-r1.png`; it is binary and solid across the S10 body. Exact mechanism: Sharp expanded the raw one-channel resized mask to three channels; the composer incorrectly indexed that RGB buffer as one alpha byte per output pixel, consuming `255,0,0` as successive pixels/rows and producing the one-visible/two-transparent-row scanline. The fix forces `greyscale()` output, asserts one sample per output pixel for both the Lanczos3 alpha mask and nearest binary support, and directly multiplies the composed alpha by the smooth mask bounded by that support. The previous `dest-in` clip is gone. F16 now measures each placed building's solid-context mapped body in the final city layer: low-coverage (<50%) rows / high-coverage (>90%) rows <= 0.05. Named results: S18 `0/340=0`; S01 `0/281=0`; S15 `0/180=0`; S10 `0/330=0`; S11 `0/221=0`; all 19 placements pass. Bundled-Node build and byte-identical `--check`, scoped ESLint, and `git diff --check` pass. No runtime, frozen-layer, kit, ImageGen, or commit change.

**Director (industry research delivered, 2026-08-26):** The owner-directed research agent returned a full dossier (saved: `.codex-tmp/qa/T18/research/water-animation-dossier.md`). THE ORGANIZING PRINCIPLE, which retroactively diagnoses every T18 texture round: "the games that best animate painted-looking water almost never move the paint — they move LIGHT, PHASE, and OVERLAYS across paint that stays put." Valve's own flow-map water never advects the base color (normals/debris only); two-phase flow on a crisp painting produces the defining mid-phase double-exposure blur we kept fighting. Top-5 by fit to our stack: (1) normal-from-luminance relighting with a traveling phase (the painting's own strokes carry moving light; zero resampling); (2) SDF shoreline foam bands + synced swash (the literal coastal brief; Cyanilux/Wind Waker/Sea-of-Thieves lineage); (3) hash-phase sparkle twinkle with zoom-constant density (also the far-zoom ocean carrier); (4) gradient-map palette cycling along the stroke field (Ferrari LivingWorlds emulated — strokes march without pixels moving); (5) two-phase flow DEMOTED to detail/foam layers only, with per-pixel time noise. Recommended composite: static painted albedo + ONE shared wave-phase field driving relight + cycling, SDF foam at coast, twinkle everywhere, demoted flow for streaks, all layers zoom-weighted (which IS the tier story). This converges exactly on T23's mask inputs — T23-r2 will implement the composite per the dossier.

**Owner defect exemplar → NEW GATE (axis coherence, 2026-08-26):** Owner annotated the r2 candidate with traced rail directions ("see") — the straight yard rails fan across multiple incompatible screen axes and the station sits on another; in this axonometric world every world-parallel line must be SCREEN-PARALLEL (no vanishing-point convergence; the generator drifts toward photographic perspective). NEW MEASURED GATE — AXIS COHERENCE: for linear features (rails first; road/wall/building edges as applicable), extract dominant stroke directions per parallel-class; all members must match the canonical iso axis within tolerance (target ±2°, calibrated against the master/D05 canon's held axes); implemented exactly as the owner's yellow-line tracing, but measured. Applies to: the T22-r3 patch outputs at director review (patched rails must be axis-true — verify before the owner sees them), the composition-fidelity checklist (with perspective consistency, per the prior F24 correction), and ALL future plate/tile generation gates. **Owner (post-r3 read #2): "while the coast has some cool animations The waves dont move" + asks the open-ocean plan of record.** Recording banked (`.codex-tmp/qa/T23/r3/owner-recording-2/`, ~29s). Director analysis: the composite animates LIGHT and PHASE (sparkle, crest glow, foam pulse) but wave-form TRAVEL isn't reading — the two techniques that carry it (gradient palette cycling marching the strokes; SDF swash bands arriving shoreward) are present but under-amplituded/under-legible. WAVE-TRAVEL LEGIBILITY is now a named acceptance criterion for the r4 director review: "can an untrained eye see crests advancing, especially shoreward?" — tune cycling amplitude/phase speed + swash band contrast first (panel dials + defaults); if legibility cannot be reached within the light/phase architecture, that is the honest signal that wave travel needs its own mechanism (e.g., a translating luminance-band layer) in an r5. OPEN-OCEAN PLAN OF RECORD (restated for the owner): NOT pre-baked — per the owner's own bake-sequencing ruling, bake only ever against frozen art as an end-game topper. The open ocean is the procedural layer MODIFIED TO MATCH: graded to the painted register (done), calm far-field with broad swells + zoom-constant glint (r7 tier scope), demoted to deep-sea supporting player between landmasses; painted animated water owns every coast and expands with each district and the post-territories terrain pass.

**Owner insight (baked-art ceiling, 2026-08-26): "idk if itll move cause its baked city art though. I turned off the ocean to showcase"** (recording banked). Confirmed and answered on the record: (1) TRUE — baked crest pixels can never translate; (2) baked art CAN still read as flowing (Ferrari's waterfalls are static pixels), but our canon water wasn't AUTHORED for cycling (no deliberate gradient runs along flow direction), so retrofitted cycling yields partial apparent motion — the honest ceiling of the light-and-phase family on this art; (3) the ARCHITECTURAL answer: the effects layer is NOT baked — swash bands already translate freely, and the r5 mechanism (if r4's tuned legibility still falls short) is TRAVELING OVERLAY WAVES: crest/foam elements authored from the painting's own material, drawn and translated by the effects layer over the static painted sea. "The painting is the sea; the overlay is the waves." No baked-art ceiling applies to the overlay. **Gate audit (F23) — the same lesson twice in one lane, and I own the first half.** (1) **The detail gate was satisfied BY the wrong content**: water sparkle and foam carry high high-frequency energy, so the river scored well on |Laplacian| and helped carry the candidate from 84.2% to 94.1%. A metric can be passed by the very thing it exists to exclude — this is "metrics are not targets" arriving one level up from where it hit T58. (2) **The masonry locator was made self-referential**: it reported `0 / 0` after its reference authority was swapped to the finished-art transfer authority, i.e. the transplanted content compared against itself, which cannot detect a transplant by construction. The worker disclosed the swap in its record rather than hiding it, and its stated reasoning (displaced pixels cannot be compared to the banned scaffold) identified a genuine problem — but the resolution was wrong. **NEW BINDING RULES: (a) CONTENT CLASS gate — a repainted scope must contain only the classes appropriate to it, measured: water `<=0.10%` against the 0.07% mounted baseline, structure/masonry components `0` by an INDEPENDENT scan. (b) GATE INDEPENDENCE — a gate's reference authority may never be derived from the candidate or from the candidate's own sources; where no valid independent reference exists, the gate is declared INAPPLICABLE and replaced with an absolute classifier, stated explicitly. Silently redefining a gate's basis is a FAIL, not an adaptation.**

**Director (T58c review: BLOCK ENDORSED — the defective rule was mine; T58d dispatched):** **T58c is the best-behaved lane in this arc and the block is fully endorsed.** It refused to draw from the 21 windows its own raw scorer nominated once direct inspection showed them to be built-city masonry; it declared CONTENT CLASS, DETAIL REGISTER, grade, rectangle and stripped-layer gates **INAPPLICABLE rather than reporting `0` against a candidate that did not exist** — the gate-independence rule I wrote one lane earlier, applied correctly and unprompted; it declined to repoint my detail script at invalid output; it emitted no misleading sheet; and it named the exact rule change it would have had to make silently in order to proceed. F24 positive exemplar, and the contrast with T57's undisclosed protection breach is the whole point of the rule.

**The defect is my source rule.** I required RAW SOURCE windows to score `>= 56.20` mean |Laplacian| — but `56.20` is the mean of a MIXED control ring, not a floor a 100x150 window of gorge rock should be expected to clear. Independently re-measured by me: E3 `[1545,553]` `57.54`, E4 `[1778,574]` `49.66`, east-gorge `[1650,600]` `54.69`, `[1670,600]` `53.86` — all with `0` blue; against urban `[1080,280]` `68.93` and `[1110,340]` `57.98`, also `0` blue. **The only windows clearing my floor were the city-masonry ones the morphology classifier false-negatived: the rule selected AGAINST legitimate rock and FOR built structure.** I also conflated a source-selection criterion with the output gate — the binding gate is on the CANDIDATE (`>= 90%` of ring), never on raw source pixels, and T58b's own history proves the distinction (direct transfer `84.2%` → residual `94.1%`).

**Classifier limitation banked:** absolute morphology masonry detection false-negatives on built-city regions. It is not sufficient alone and is now paired with a region exclusion plus a mandatory visual inspection sheet of every chosen source window — direct inspection is what saved this lane and it is no longer discretionary.

**My packet contained three defective gates.**

**(1) The depth measurement sampled the wrong pixels.** I specified a fixed 60px column between crown and footing — but the DECK is drawn over the face, so the upper part of that column is track and ballast, not aperture interior. **E2's `68.7` "upper aperture" reading was measuring rails.** The reference values I derived for the crossing arches (`37.3/31.3`, `48.6/43.8`) came from the same flawed method and are WITHDRAWN. The visual finding stands — the openings do read as filled at native zoom — but the numbers I attached to it did not measure what I said they measured. **Correction: measure only genuinely transparent aperture pixels (the lane's own `12,167`-pixel set), split by pixel count, with the identical definition applied to the crossing openings.**

**(2) The protected train rect is a bounding box that is roughly half empty, and it made the gate unsatisfiable.** T59c found E2's upper band `[1214,694,1274,733]` lay `1,042/1,042` inside the immutable rect. My own inspection (`.codex-tmp/qa/T59/c/director-trainrect-halves.png`) shows why that is absurd: **the WEST half of `[1199,593,1555,759]` contains no train at all** — rails, ballast, deck cargo and a gold finial — while the locomotive and carriage sit entirely in the EAST half. The rect has been carried as sacred since T56 and it over-protects by roughly half its area, including a large part of the E2 opening. **Correction: derive the TRUE train protection from actual train art plus a stated margin; the old rect is superseded as a protection but RETAINED as the reporting rect, split into inside-true-mask (must be `0`) and outside-mask-inside-old-rect (may be non-zero, stated), so the number stays comparable across the arc. This does NOT retroactively excuse T57**, whose changed region covered real locomotive art and which failed by not reporting the protection at all.

**(3) The near-black gate was absolute against a non-zero baseline — the T56 error, repeated by me after I logged it.** I required `0` pixels below luma `24`; the existing baseline in the legally mutable aperture area measures `3,601`. **This is F27 verbatim: calibrate against the accepted baseline. I wrote that rule into this file after T56 and then broke it four lanes later.** Correction: the gate is no INCREASE over baseline.

**Pattern worth stating plainly:** across T56→T59c the recurring failure is not worker execution but director gates that were never checked against ground truth before being made binding — an absolute locator the baseline failed, a scaffold named as a pixel source, a metric a river could pass, a source floor that selected for buildings, a geometry describing a face that did not exist, a measurement band sampling the deck, and a protection rectangle half of which protects nothing. **Standing rule, stronger than F27: every gate must be RUN AGAINST THE MOUNTED BASELINE and shown to pass there before it is made binding on a candidate. A gate the ground truth fails is a defect in the gate.**

**Director (owner is right; the underlay is TILED and SEAMED; my tiling check was run at the wrong zoom):** Stage-by-stage crop at 6x (`.codex-tmp/qa/T59/d/director-stage-compare-E1.png`) localises it: **the rectangles and the repeated rock motif are already present in the T58e TERRAIN**, before the face and before the depth pass. The cause was structurally guaranteed and disclosed to me at the time: **T58d/T58e filled a `26,018px` scope from only TWO `100x150` source windows, offset-repeated** — roughly `1.7x` the total source area, so visible repeats and patch boundaries were inevitable. I endorsed that source-selection call (preferring two inspected-safe windows over four uninspected ones, which was right on content grounds) without asking whether two windows could cover the area without reading as tile.

**Why my gates missed it, three ways.** (1) My tiling inspection ran at **2x** on a wide band (`director-tiling-check.png`) and I concluded "no visible repeat" — at 6x the repeat and the seams are unmistakable. **A repeat/seam check is a HIGH-ZOOM check; low zoom hides exactly the defect it is meant to find.** (2) The lanes' ownership-rectangle scans returned `0` straight-edge runs, and my own straight-edge detector cannot separate quilt seams from legitimate architecture — piers and voussoirs are straight by design, and mounted itself scores 13 vertical-edge columns in this region. **That gate is not fit for purpose and should not be cited as evidence again until it is content-aware.** (3) Every numeric register gate — detail, contrast, content class, grade — is a DISTRIBUTION statistic and is blind to spatial arrangement: identical tiles repeated produce perfect distribution scores. **No aggregate statistic can detect tiling; only a spatial/structural check or an eye at native zoom can.**

**The comparison that matters, in the owner's own framing:** the crossing's openings show a simple coherent field — water, a bank, a few rocks — while ours show a busy, repeated, seamed rock quilt. Cleanliness here is a property of COMPOSITION, not of register.

**Why no gate caught it, and this is the general lesson.** Every gate in this arc measured properties OF THE CHANGED REGION — register, continuity, tiling, near-black — and every one of them asked "is what we painted good?" **None asked "were we entitled to paint here at all?"** The protection list was the only mechanism that could have caught it, and the deck cargo was never a named protection because I had defined it as aperture. **NEW BINDING GATE — BUILT-CONTENT PRESERVATION: `0` pixels of the mounted terrain base's deck / cargo / rail / lamp content may be overwritten, measured with a stated and control-validated classifier. And the standing rule behind it: a mask derived from one layer's alpha is a claim about THAT layer only; before using it as a claim about what lies BEHIND a structure, verify against the layer that actually holds the content.**

Baseline-first measurements ran and passed before construction.

**The failure is in my packet, and the mechanism is now proven.** I told T64 to source the crates from T62's **mounted-terrain** built-content mask. Director probe of layer ownership across five crate sample points shows the cluster is **SPLIT ACROSS TWO LAYERS** — `[1215,720]`, `[1235,725]` and `[1225,745]` are overlay-opaque (`a255` in both the mounted and T59b overlays) while `[1255,715]` and `[1270,730]` are overlay-transparent and terrain-owned. **A terrain-only removal therefore deletes part of the cluster, leaves the overlay-owned part standing, and fills the gap between them with gorge field** — visible in `.codex-tmp/qa/T64/E2-crown-blocked-before-after-6x.png` as a teal blob among intact crates, which is precisely the owner's original complaint reproduced. **T64's `33.3704` continuity failure is a true measurement of that half-removal, not a property of the removal itself.** A two-layer object cannot be removed from one layer.

**Standing rule, and the third time this arc has been bitten by the same class of mistake:** *the aperture mask was a claim about one layer; the built-content mask was a claim about one layer; now the crate removal was scoped to one layer.* **Before acting on any object, establish which LAYERS own its pixels — a mask from a single layer describes that layer only.**

**The mechanism, now proven by that evidence: THE CRATE ART IS DUPLICATED IN BOTH LAYERS.** The terrain base carries its own copy of the crates underneath the overlay's copy, so clearing overlay alpha simply reveals the terrain's crates. **My T65 packet said "overlay pixels become transparent, terrain pixels are replaced" as though the two sets were disjoint. They overlap** — and the terrain copy spans the whole C1 footprint, not merely the `262px` a classifier could see, because paint hidden under an opaque overlay is invisible to any classifier reading the composite.

**F24 — the same root error, now three variants deep, all mine:** aperture mask scoped to one layer (swept in the deck); crate removal scoped to one layer (T64); crate removal treating two layers as disjoint (T65). **The generalised rule is stronger than "check which layers own it": layers may hold REDUNDANT COPIES of the same object, and a classifier reading the composite cannot see paint hidden beneath an opaque layer. Establish ownership by probing each layer independently, never by inference from what is visible.**
